## Document 013: 2026-09-30_［みずほ経済EXPRESS］中国が住宅販売モデルを転換へ_ef0b0461

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026-09-30_［みずほ経済EXPRESS］中国が住宅販売モデルを転換へ_ef0b0461.pdf
Report source: Mizuho
Date: 2026-09-30
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1

みずほ経済 EXPRESS

中国が住宅販売モデルを転換へ 
～不動産不況は脱却の兆しなお見えず～

調査部 主任エコノミスト 月岡直樹  
080-1069-6684 naoki.tsukioka@mizuho-rt.co.jp 
■ 中国政府が新規則を発表。住宅は予約販売から完成後販売にシフト 
中国政府は8月28日、住宅販売制度と住宅ローン管理に関する新規則を発表した。新規の住宅不動産
プロジェクトについて完成後販売の方式を促すほか、未完成物件の予約販売における住宅ローンの実
行時期を物件引き渡し後に変更する。住宅の予約販売制度が不動産ディベロッパーの過度なレバレッ
ジ追求による資金繰り悪化と、それに伴う住宅引き渡し遅延問題を引き起こしたことから、住宅販売
モデルの転換を加速させる。 
住宅都市農村建設部や中国人民銀行、国家金融監督管理総局などの関連省庁が一斉に通達や管理規
定を公布した。住宅販売制度に関する通達では、①予約販売の代金（購入者が支払う頭金と銀行から
振り込まれる住宅ローン）について住宅不動産プロジェクトごとの専用口座を通じた銀行側による資
金管理を強化すること、②新たに不動産用地や建設許可を取得するプロジェクトに対して「完成物件
での販売を優先的に選択」させ、建設許可を取得済みの新規プロジェクトについても完成物件での販
売を奨励すること、③住宅不動産プロジェクトごとに会社を設立し、メインバンクを選定して開発資
金の融資を受けること、などの方針を示した。一方、住宅ローンの新たな管理規定には、予約販売に
おける住宅ローンについて「厳格にプロジェクト竣工届出後に実行しなければならない」と明記した
ほか、これまで最長30年とされてきた住宅ローンの借入期間を40年に変更することを盛り込んだ。 
■ 「保交房」に一定のメド。ディベロッパーの法的整理が進む可能性も 
中国の不動産市場はこれまで、ディベロッパーが物件の竣工を待たずに販売を開始する予約販売方
式が一般的であり、住宅ローンも購入者が予約購入した時点で実行され、返済義務が生じる慣行とな
っていた。予約販売には、購入者にとって不動産価格の値上がり前に物件を確保できるメリットが、
ディベロッパーにとって不動産開発に投じた資金を早期に回収できるメリットがあった。 
しかし、財務レバレッジの追求による収益の最大化を狙うディベロッパーは、回収した予約販売代
金を新たな不動産開発用地の獲得や不動産とは関係のないビジネスへの投資に流用し、事業を急拡大
させた。その実態は自転車操業であり、市場の過熱を懸念した中国政府が2021年にディベロッパーへ
の資金調達規制 （いわゆる 「3つのレッドライン」 ） を正式導入するとたちまち資金繰りに行き詰まり、
社債の債務不履行（デフォルト）が頻発する。ディベロッパーの信用力が低下したところにゼロコロ
2026 年 9 月 30 日

### Page 2

2

ナ政策の強化が追い打ちをかける形で販売が急減し、ディベロッパーの資金繰りが一段と悪化したこ
とで建設工事の進捗が滞り、2022年に入って予約販売済み物件の引き渡し遅延が深刻化する。 
ここに至って予約販売のデメリットが明らかとなる。すなわち、物件引き渡しリスクを負担するの
は購入者であり、たとえ遅延しても住宅ローンの返済を続けなければならないことである。事態を重
く見た中国政府は、「保交楼（予約販売済み未完成物件の引き渡し保証。「保交房」も同じ）」のス
ローガンを掲げ、ディベロッパーの資金繰り支援に乗り出す。2024年1月には、当局が融資適格と判断
した不動産開発プロジェクトをまとめたホワイトリストを作成し、各銀行がこのリストに基づいてデ
ィベロッパーに資金を融通するスキームを構築して、問題の解消を急がせたのである。 
中国政府がこのタイミングで新規則を発表した背景には、この「保交房」に一定のメドが付いたと
判断したことがあるとみられる。事実、国務院の李強首相は2026年3月の全人代における政府活動報告
で「『保交房』の任務は全面的に完成した」と宣言している。また、見逃せない動きとして、広東省
広州市の中級人民法院（地方裁判所）が8月21日、実質的に経営破綻している某ディベロッパーについ
て、債権者による清算の申し立てを受理したことが挙げられる。 
デフォルトを起こした不動産ディベロッパーの多くはこれまで、倒産によって債務の返済や予約販
売物件の引き渡し、建設業者への代金支払いなどの義務・責任を放棄することが許されず、債権者か
ら債務の減免や返済猶予の合意を得つつゾンビ化した状態で経営を続けてきた。ディベロッパーの破
綻が相次げば、不良債権問題の深刻化によって金融システムが動揺するリスクを招くだけでなく、予
約販売物件の引き渡しが困難となって社会問題に発展したり、下請け業者の連鎖倒産で景気が急激に
冷え込む可能性もあったからである。 
今回の動きは、清算の申し立てを裁判所が「受理」しただけではあり、実際に清算が進むのかはな
お予断を許さないが、中国政府がディベロッパーの法的整理を前に進める意思を示したとみることも
できる。今後、清算手続きが順調に進むのか、他のディベロッパーについても同様の動きが広がるの
かを注意深く見守る必要があろう。 
■ 住宅販売モデル転換の社会的意義は大きいが、不動産市況への影響は軽微 
では、住宅販売モデルの転換は不動産市場にどのような影響を与えるであろうか。その前にまず、
不動産市場の現状を整理しておきたい。 
資金調達規制の強化をきっかけとした不動産不況は6年目に突入した。中国の不動産市場規模（販売
金額）は2021～25年にかけて半分に縮小し、販売面積や開発投資額は4割、新規着工面積に至っては7
割も減少した（図表1）。2026年も不況脱却の兆しは見えず、1～8月の販売面積は前年比▲12.1％、開
発投資額は同▲19.9％と低迷が続いている。開発投資の先行指標とされる不動産開発向け銀行貸出も
同▲33.3％となっており、ディベロッパーにとって資金繰りが厳しい状況は変わっていない。 
販売が大きく落ち込んだことで、市場には過剰な在庫が積み上がっている。みずほ総合研究所調査
部の試算によれば、建設中の物件を含む中国の住宅在庫（商用およびオフィス用を除く）は2025年末
時点で40.8億平米に達する（図表2）。2025年の住宅販売面積が7.3億平米であったことから、新規着工
がゼロになると仮定しても在庫をすべて消化するのに5年以上かかる計算である。中国政府は2024年5
月、地方政府による住宅在庫の買い取り策を発表し、同年10月には地方政府が専項債（特別債）の発
行で得た資金を買い取りに使うことも容認したが、在庫の消化は遅々として進んでいない。

### Page 3

3

住宅価格（主要70都市平均）は2021年7月のピーク時から新築で▲14％、中古で▲23％下落し、全体
として下落傾向に歯止めがかかっていない。ただ、図表3のとおり、底堅い需要のある大都市（一級都
市：北京、上海、広州、深圳）と膨大な在庫を抱える地方都市（二級都市：各省の省都。三級都市：
省都以下の主要都市）で二極化が顕著になっている。特に上海は堅調で、その新築住宅価格は2021年
7月から＋24％も上昇している。とはいえ、その堅調さは資産としての不動産保有の魅力を有する一部
の都市に限ったものであり、市況の回復が地方都市にまで広く波及する様子はうかがえない。 
住宅引き渡し遅延問題の発生以降、引き渡しリスクのない完成物件の購入が増加しつつあるが、予
約販売は2025年時点でも依然として住宅販売面積全体の6割超を占めている（図表4）。住宅販売モデ
ルの転換、すなわち予約販売から完成後販売への移行と予約販売における住宅ローン実行の後ろ倒し
は、住宅購入者の物件引き渡しリスクを大幅に軽減することになり、消費者保護の観点からもその社
会的意義は非常に大きいといえる。しかし、これに伴ってディベロッパーの資金繰りはより厳しいも
のとなる。ディベロッパーの資金調達に占める販売代金（頭金などの前受金と住宅ローン）の比率は
4割を超えているからである（図表5）。このため、短期的には新規の開発（土地の取得や新規着工）
図表1 不動産指標（年次） 図表2 住宅在庫

が減少する可能性が高い。ただ、新規則は完成
後販売を全国一律に「強制」するのではなく、
あくまで「優先的に選択」させるとしているこ
とから、地方によってはディベロッパーの資金
繰りにも配慮した柔軟な対応がとられる可能
性もある。 
新規制が不動産市況に与える影響は、住宅ロ
ーンの最長借入期間の延長が新たな需要を掘
り起こすとしても、軽微であろう。不動産不況
を脱するためには、過剰在庫の消化とディベロ
ッパーの法的整理、不良債権の処理を着実に進
め、市場を健全な状態に戻すことが不可欠とい
えるが、それにはまだ相当の時間を要するもの
とみられる。

2  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
上週重要市場資訊
⚫ 美國 9 月 ISM 非製造業 PMI 從 8 月的 55.4 降至 54.9，顯示服務業活動增速放
緩，然而國內需求強勁使供應鏈承壓，並推動投入價格指數由 72.6 升至 74.0 為
四年多來新高；另一方面，美國 10 月密西根大學消費者信心指數由 9 月的 48.1
降至 46.3，為連續第三個月下降且創 5 月以來最低 ， 惟未來一年通膨預期由 4.6%
升至 4.7%，顯示通膨可能持續維持高位至 2027 年。
⚫ 聯準會 9 月 FOMC 會議紀要顯示與會官員均支持升息，並認為年底前尚需升息
一次 ，但對於後續升息時點及升息理由上存在分歧； 其中部分官員認為升息是防
止能源及其他價格衝擊造成更廣泛影響 的預防性措施，但更為鷹派官員則認為
升息在於抑制投資和支出，以防範正在顯現的需求驅動型通膨。
⚫ 歐洲央行 9 月貨幣政策會議紀要顯示全體決策委員均同意升息，並認為當前經
濟成長風險偏向下行 ， 通膨風險則偏向上行 ，因此將保留政策彈性， 根據經濟數

據做為決策，不提供未來利率路徑指引。
⚫ 台灣 9 月 CPI 年增率自 8 月 2.05%上升至 2.73%，已連續 5 個月超過 2%通膨警
戒線 ， 且創兩年半來最大漲幅 ，除了豪雨導致蔬果價格上漲及中秋假期的短期因
素外，油價居高不下亦使通膨壓力增加。
⚫ 中國央行發布匯率政策立場聲明， 強調中國貿易發展根源於產業國際競爭力的
提升，無必要通過匯率貶值獲取貿易競爭優勢 ，並指出中國長期實施以市場供需
為基礎、參考一籃子貨幣進行調節管理浮動匯率制度。

### Page 3

3  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
 美元兌新台幣匯率走勢  
Oct/12/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週台幣匯價高點在 31.699，低點在 31.940，以 31.875 作收，較前一週收盤價
貶值 4.5 分。 
上週台幣震盪走貶，週初，受到前週公布的美國非農就業數據低於預期，降低
市場對聯準會 10 月升息的預期， 台股創高吸引外資匯入 ， 推升台幣最高來到31.699；
不過隨著美伊緊張情勢升溫且台灣連假將至 ， 台股收低使外資轉為大幅賣超並匯出 ，
推低台幣回到 9 月中以來的震盪區間下緣，最低觸及 31.940，最終在央行調節之下
收在 31.875。 
＜未來分析＞ 
    預估本週台幣區間震盪。就國際市場而言，美國 10 月密西根大學消費者信心
指數持續下滑，由 9 月的 48.1 降至 46.3，創 5 月以來最低，惟未來一年通膨預期
升至 4.7%，加上中東局勢反覆，油價高位震盪，仍支撐市場對於聯準會年內升息
的預期，預計美元指數維持在 102 附近震盪，對台幣帶來壓力；就台灣市場來看，
台灣 9 月出口創歷史新高，年增 60.9%，反映 AI 及科技需求持續強勁，出口商後
續拋匯需求可望提供台幣支撐；然而，台股逼近五萬點關卡之際，外資未能延續買
超走勢，且連假前已大幅賣超台股，若後續外資持續匯出，將限制台幣升值空間。
綜合以上，預估本週台幣區間震盪。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌台幣：31.700-32.000

上週人民幣區間震盪 。 中國十一長假期間 (10/1~10/7) 境內人民幣市場休市 ， 而
境外人民幣維持偏強格局 ， 主要在6.7000 上方震盪 ， 臨近周末 ， 人行中間價為6.7330，
創 3 年多來新高，加上美元指數漲勢放緩，推升境外人民幣突破 6.7000 整數關卡，
終場境外人民幣收在 6.6927，週線升值 134 點，境內人民幣收在 6.6929，較十一長
假前夕升值 121 點。

＜未來分析＞ 
    預估本週人民幣區間偏升。就國際市場來看，美國密西根大學消費者信心指數
由 9 月的 48.1 降至 46.3，創5 月以來新低 ， 不過對於未來一年的通膨預期升至4.7%，
加上油價維持高檔 ， 市場對於聯準會年內升息的預期仍存 ， 美元指數維持高檔震盪 ，
將限制人民幣升值速度；就中國國內市場來看，人行中間價維持偏強設定，不過人
行表示將防範短期大幅波動與急貶風險 ， 並無意透過人民幣貶值取得貿易競爭優勢 ，
有助於穩定市場對人民幣的預期 ；最後就技術面來看 ，USD/CNH 日線圖中 ， 各天期
均線仍向下發散，空頭格局不變，下方支撐先看向 9 月底低點 6.6908。綜合以上，
預估本週人民幣區間偏升。

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週 USD/JPY 震盪走升。週初，因美元指數在102 附近盤整，帶領USD/JPY 延
續前一週走勢震盪走升到 158 上方；週間，因缺乏重要經濟數據，市場偏向觀望，
USD/JPY 維持在 158 附近震盪；臨近週末，USD/JPY 曾一度回測 157.50 附近，不
過在美日利差的主導下，USD/JPY 依舊保持偏多的走勢，終場收在 158.31，週線上
漲 84 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
預估本週 USD/JPY 區間震盪，主要原因如下：一、路透短觀調查顯示，受半導
體強勁訂單推動，日本 10 月大型製造業信心升至正 22，創近五年新高；但原料成
本高漲重擊非製造業，信心指數降至正 23。與此同時，此前投下升息反對票的日銀
鴿派委員佐藤綾野立場軟化，表態支持分階段升息，市場對後續正常化預期升溫，
預計將限縮 USD/JPY 的上行空間 。二 、 日本首相高市早苗表示密切關注匯率與物價
走勢 ， 強調日本已脫離通縮 ， 將完全尊重央行政策獨立性 ， 暗示不反對進一步升息 。
面對通膨加劇與日圓走貶壓力，其過往堅持的貨幣寬鬆立場已出現轉向。然而目前
美日實質利差仍大，持續為 USD/JPY 提供支撐。三、就技術面而言，日線圖中，
USD/JPY 持續沿著上彎的月線上攻，然匯價持續承壓於季線下方，在有效站上季線
及突破前高壓力前，仍以區間震盪看待。綜合以上，預估USD/JPY 本週呈現區間震
盪的可能性較高。

＜上週走勢＞ 
  上週 EUR/USD 震盪 走跌 。週初， 因法國債券風險與區域政治不確定性 ，
EUR/USD 下行最低來到 1.1160； 週間 ，受中東地緣風險升溫與歐洲財政疑慮帶動避
險需求 ， 加上美債殖利率維持高檔 ， 美元利差優勢延續，歐元維持低檔； 臨近週末 ，
逢低歐元有買盤支撐，帶動歐元收復部分跌幅，終場收在 1.1201，週線下跌 46 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 EUR/USD 區間偏空震盪，主要原因如下：一、歐洲央行首席經濟學
家連恩表示，夏末能源成本飆升為經濟增長帶來下行風險，並可能限制央行收緊貨
幣政策的力道，與此同時，歐元區 10 月 Sentix 投資者信心指數由 5.1 降至 2.7，德
國預期指數同步滑落，反映市場對經濟復甦的信心明顯降溫，預計將令歐元持續承
壓。二、法國社會動盪與支出訴求阻礙財政減赤進程，加劇債務可持續性疑慮，並
推升法德公債利差與信用風險，令歐元承壓走弱。相較美國經濟展現韌性且聯準會
維持偏鷹立場，歐洲深陷景氣放緩與財政掣肘，歐洲央行緊縮空間受限。在歐美基
本面與政策前景持續分歧下 ，EUR/USD 缺乏實質支撐 ， 後市將維持偏弱整理並下探
關鍵支撐。三、技術面而言，在日線圖中，EUR/USD 匯價雖多次向下測試 1.1165 關
鍵價位均獲得支撐，惟每次反彈均無法站上五日線，且各天期均線下彎呈現空頭排
列。綜合以上，預估 EUR/USD 本週呈現區間偏空震盪的可能性較高。

＜預估本週＞ 歐元兌美元：1.1100-1.1260

持偏緊的貨幣政策，加上國際債市利率持續走升，台債殖利率恐面臨上行壓
力，預估 5 年券區間為 1.85%~1.95%，10 年券區間為 2.0%~2.10%。

1

國際經濟金融週報內容摘要 
美 
國 
美國服務業與內需維持擴張，通膨黏著與中東風險牽動政策前景 
經濟 
⚫ 9 月美國 ISM 服務業 PMI 由 55.4 降至 54.9，連續第 27 個月維持擴張。
商業活動及新訂單雖較前月回落 ， 仍保持穩健成長 ； 就業重返擴張區間 ，
內需與勞動市場仍具韌性 。 惟價格指數續升創高 ， 出口與進口動能轉弱 ，
後續仍須關注通膨壓力及外需變化。 
⚫ 8 月美國商品及服務貿易逆差擴大至 1,056 億美元 ， 較上月增加13.7%。
出口 3,152 億美元、進口4,208 億美元，分別月增1.4%及 4.3%。出口成
長主要受非貨幣黃金、原油及半導體帶動，進口則以原油、黃金及資本
財增加為主 。 累計前8 月貿易逆差 5,570 億美元 ， 較去年同期縮減19.9%，
惟近期進口增幅高於出口，使逆差再度擴大。 
⚫ 9 月 FOMC 會議紀要顯示，AI 投資持續帶動經濟與就業動能，勞動市
場仍具韌性；惟去通膨進展有限，核心服務通膨及成本轉嫁壓力仍存。
全體官員支持升息 1 碼，傾向維持限制性政策，並關注 AI 融資、公債
市場波動及網路安全等金融風險。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 近期美伊緊張情勢再升溫，川普改口不排除選前對伊朗採取軍事行動，
伊朗則持續尋求重啟談判；同時葉門青年運動頻繁襲擊沙國目標，加劇
中東安全風險。若衝突擴大，恐影響荷姆茲與曼德海峽能源運輸，推升
油氣價格並增添全球經濟下行風險。 
⚫ 美國啟動 337 調查，針對 AI 伺服器垂直供電系統（VPD）相關專利侵
權爭議展開審查，鴻海、台達電、廣達等約 20 家業者列名。由於涉及
AI 伺服器關鍵供電技術，若最終認定侵權，相關產品恐面臨輸美限制，
對供應鏈營運帶來影響。 
短評 
⚫ 美國服務業與內需仍具韌性，AI 投資亦支撐經濟動能，惟貿易逆差擴
大、成本壓力升高及通膨黏著，使聯準會仍需維持偏緊縮立場。加上美
伊與中東局勢升溫，能源價格與金融市場波動風險上升，後續景氣不確
定性仍高。

115.10.12 
一、 美國—美國服務業與內需維持擴張，通膨黏著與中東風險牽動政策前景 
2026 年 9 月美國 ISM 服務業採購經理人指數(Services PMI) 為 54.9，較上月
55.4 下降 0.5 個百分點，已連續第 27 個月維持於 50 以上擴張區間，且高於近 12
個月平均值 54.1，服務業景氣仍持續穩健擴張。細項觀察，商業活動指數由 61.7
降至 56.5，新訂單指數由 60.9 降至 59.8，雖較前月回落，但仍維持高檔擴張；就
業指數則由 47.8 回升至 50.1，三個月來首度重返擴張區間。價格指數由 72.6 升
至 74.0，創 2022 年 7 月以來新高。供應商交貨指數由 51.3 升至 53.2，交貨時間
再度延長；庫存指數由56.7 升至 57.8，企業持續提前備貨以因應未來成本上升風
險。此外，未完成訂單指數升至 56.6，創 2022 年 7 月以來高點，惟新出口訂單
指數由 56.3 大幅降至 46.9，八個月來首度跌入收縮區間，進口指數亦由 56.3 降
至 52.9，外部需求及採購動能均降溫。整體而言，美國服務業景氣仍維持穩健擴
張，內需與就業展現一定韌性，但燃料價格上漲、關稅政策、供應鏈限制及地緣
政治等因素持續推升企業成本，後續仍須關注通膨壓力及外需變化對景氣的影響。 
2026 年 8 月美國商品及服務貿易逆差擴大至 1,056 億美元 ， 較上月修正後928
億美元增加 127 億美元，增幅 13.7%。當月出口 3,152 億美元，較上月增加 45 億
美元(+1.4%)；進口 4,208 億美元，較上月增加 172 億美元(+4.3%)。其中，商品貿
易逆差擴大至 1,366 億美元，服務貿易順差則小幅增至 310 億美元。出口成長主
要受工業用品及原材料出口增加 63 億美元帶動，其中非貨幣黃金、原油及燃油
出口增幅較為顯著，資本財出口亦受半導體、電腦及電腦周邊設備需求增加所支
撐；進口方面，工業用品及原材料進口增加 91 億美元，資本財進口增加 62 億美
元，其中原油、非貨幣黃金、半導體及工業機械進口增幅較大。累計2026 年 1 至
8 月，美國商品及服務貿易逆差為 5,570 億美元，較 2025 年同期減少 1,382 億美
元，縮減 19.9%；同期出口成長 11.8%，進口增加 4.4%，顯示美國對外貿易失衡
情況較去年同期有所改善，惟近期進口增幅明顯高於出口，使貿易逆差再度呈現
擴大趨勢。 
2026 年 9 月 FOMC 會議紀要重點如下：(1)經濟：若干官員認為美國經濟成
長動能有所增強 ，AI 投資規模與速度持續超乎預期 ， 帶動企業投資與生產力提升 ；

用相關內容。

5

企業活動則受到企業獲利維持高檔及部分政策因素支持；(2)就業：多數官員表示
勞動市場近期略有改善，就業成長維持穩健，失業率仍處低檔，整體勞動市場大
致符合充分就業狀態，供需風險大致平衡；(3)通膨：官員普遍認為近期去通膨進
展有限，非住房核心服務通膨仍偏高，人力、能源及部分進口成本轉嫁情形仍值
得關注；部分官員擔憂 AI 投資與相關支出擴張，可能推升需求並增加通膨持續
性風險；(4)貨幣政策：鑑於經濟活動持續擴張、勞動市場具韌性且通膨仍高於目
標，全體官員支持升息 1 碼。許多官員認為維持較具限制性的政策立場有助降低
通膨風險，部分官員則認為未來可能仍需進一步緊縮政策，以確保通膨回到目標
水準；(5)金融風險：官員普遍認為金融條件整體仍支持經濟成長，但部分官員關
切 AI 基礎設施快速擴張所衍生的融資風險，以及美國公債市場波動、網路安全
與其他潛在金融穩定風險。 
近期美伊及中東局勢再度升溫，市場對區域衝突擴大的疑慮升高。美國總統
川普由先前宣稱期中選舉前不會攻擊伊朗 ， 改口表示 「仍有可能」 採取軍事行動 ，
且美軍已就相關軍事選項進行準備，使美伊對抗風險再度攀升；另一方面，伊朗
雖有意透過斡旋重啟談判，但雙方在核計畫與制裁議題上仍存重大分歧。此外，
葉門青年運動持續升高對沙烏地阿拉伯攻擊，利雅德哈立德國王國際機場一週內
多次遇襲，造成多人死傷，凸顯區域安全風險升高。整體而言，若美伊衝突或代
理人戰事進一步升級，恐衝擊荷姆茲海峽及曼德海峽等能源運輸要道，推升國際
油氣價格、加劇通膨壓力，並增添全球金融市場波動及經濟成長下行風險。 
美國國際貿易委員會(USITC)於 10 月 9 日正式啟動 337 條款調查，針對特定
垂直供電系統(Vertical Power Delivery，VPD)、 相關零組件及採用該技術的運算設
備是否涉及專利侵權展開審查。此案由美國電源技術廠商 Vicor Corporation 提出
申訴，主張相關產品侵犯其專利權，並要求發布有限排除令及停止侵權命令，以
限制涉案產品進入美國市場 。 調查對象涵蓋全球約20 家企業 ， 包括台灣的鴻海 、
台達電、廣達及其美國關係企業，另有中國的立訊精密、工業富聯、德國的英飛
凌等國際大廠列名 。 本案聚焦 AI 伺服器及高效能運算領域關鍵的供電架構技術 ，
該技術有助提升晶片供電效率、降低能源損耗，已廣泛應用於資料中心與 AI 運
算設備。隨著全球 AI 基礎建設投資持續擴大，相關專利布局與授權爭議亦日益
升溫。USITC 指出，此次立案僅代表調查程序正式展開，並不表示已認定侵權，
後續將由行政法官進行聽證及審理，預計於 45 天內訂定調查時程。台灣經濟部

則表示，337 調查屬企業間智慧財產權爭議，政府將持續關注案情發展，並於必
要時提供業者相關協助 ； 若最終認定違反337 條款 ， 涉案產品可能面臨輸美限制 ，
對 AI 伺服器供應鏈帶來潛在影響。 
二、歐元區—法國殖利率短期內恐難降溫 
 德國工業生產在 7 月月減 1.2%後，8 月回彈 2%，主要反映部分汽車業者在 7
月提前進行歲修 ， 並且在8 月陸續復工後 ， 帶動生產及庫存回補需求所致 ； 此外 ，
受到 8 月萊茵河水位降低，沿岸化學工廠物流運輸受阻，致化工產業產出月減
1.2%為主要拖累因素。儘管工業生產如期反彈，惟工業訂單卻大幅下跌 10.6%，
主要是與軍工產業相關的訂單如飛機、船舶、軍用車輛、武器彈藥等訂單減少，
顯示政府前期大額採購效應逐漸消退；除軍工產業外，其餘產業訂單則大致維持
穩定。 
 法國 8 月工業生產月減 0.3%，為連續第二個月下滑，主要係夏季用電高峰過
後電力產出回落，帶動能源部門生產減少所致。另方面，製造業生產月增0.3%，
其中造紙、化工及電子電機設備等產業自前期低點回升，部分抵銷汽車等運輸設
備產出月減 2.2%的拖累。不過，法國 9 月 INSEE 新訂單指標由 8 月的-13.8 點降
至-15.8 點，顯示企業接單偏弱，主要反映出口需求疲軟。此外，相較於德國近期
透過國防及基礎建設支出擴大支撐工業生產，法國財政擴張力道相對有限，使製
造業景氣較依賴外部需求，德、法兩國製造業表現因而呈現逐漸分化的趨勢。 
 展望未來，近期歐元區各國殖利率上升，除反映通膨壓力增強、升息預增加
之外，亦部分反映西班牙宣布於 11 月 29 日提前進行大選、法國財政前景不明朗
等政治因素影響。儘管較高的殖利率有助於促使法國政府推動財政整頓，但考量
相關改革涉及福利及退休制度調整等政治敏感議題，不確定性較高，短期內德國
與法國殖利率差距恐難縮小。整體而言，法國製造業在出口需求疲弱及財政支撐
相對有限下，景氣復甦力道恐仍較為溫和，預料德、法兩國經濟及工業活動表現
分化的情形短期內仍將持續。 
三、日本—薪資成長支撐內需，消費信心持穩 
 8 月薪資年增 3.8%，其中經常性薪資年增 3.8%、加班費年增 5.2%、實質薪資
年增 1.5%， 主要受惠於春鬥加薪效應延續及通膨壓力較去年趨緩所帶動 ， 有助於
進一步提升民眾購買力，支撐消費與經濟復甦。

9 月消費者信心指數小幅下滑 0.1 點至 35.4，但表現仍優於市場預期。受食品
及商品價格持續上漲影響 ， 民眾對未來一年通膨的預期升溫 ， 預期通膨率高於2%
的家庭比例升至 79.3%，並使生活水準及耐久財購買意願略為轉弱。不過，穩健
的薪資成長及股市上漲帶來的金融收入，持續支撐家庭所得，有助於緩衝物價上
漲對消費的負面影響，顯示日本內需仍具一定韌性。 
四、中國—歐中達成貿易談判，外部風險暫時降溫 
中國 9 月外匯存底降至 3.40 兆美元，較前月減少 381 億美元，反映美元升
值、政府以外匯購買黃金儲備，加上歐美殖利率上升，造成中國持有的海外債券
資產出現評價損失，皆為外匯存底下滑的主要原因。惟亦不排除中國外匯流入情
況可能有所轉弱，例如出口成長放緩、進口需求增加，或資本外流規模高於預期
等因素。 
展望未來 ， 近期歐中談判達成16 項共識 ， 未來中國混合動力車出口至歐盟將
面臨調整壓力，但歐盟進一步升高貿易保護措施的風險已降溫。另一方面，基於
中國維持大額經常帳順差、出口商結匯需求及年底季節性資金流入支撐，預期人
民幣仍有升值空間，並帶動外匯存底回升。 
五、印度及東南亞—世銀上調經濟展望；印度央行近四年來首度升息 
(1)世界銀行 2026 年 10 月《東亞與太平洋地區經濟展望報告》指出，受人工智慧
（AI） 相關出口與投資強勁帶動，將2026 年東亞與太平洋地區 （EAP） 經濟成長
率預測上修 0.3 個百分點至 4.5%，2027 年與 2028 年將分別放緩至 4.4%與 4.3%，
其中，越南、馬來西亞、泰國 GDP 大幅上調至 7.4%、5.1%及 2.0%。世銀指出，
AI 相關商品占多數經濟體出口增長的一半以上，其中，馬來西亞、菲律賓、泰國
和越南的 AI 出口貢獻率更超過七成，但扣除 AI 相關產品後，區域貿易成長呈現
疲弱或負增長，警告該地區的經濟韌性高度依賴 AI 相關製造業和出口，使其容
易受到全球科技支出逆轉的影響，建議應將 AI 產品生產優勢轉化為廣泛應用，
以提升生產力，並創造更多優質就業機會。此外，美伊戰爭推升能源價格，各國
政府比其他地區更積極因應 ， 也更仰賴補貼 ， 此將增加財政成本並減少外匯存底 ，
世銀預估中東石油出口要到 2027 年中才會恢復至戰前水準，能源成本上升已對
製造業造成壓力 ， 運輸價格大漲也已打擊消費需求 ， 持續性的補貼可能難以為繼 。

(2)印度:印度央行 10/7 貨幣政策會議宣布升息 1 碼至 5.5%，為近四年來首度升
息，貨幣政策立場從「中性」調整為「有節制的緊縮」。央行總裁指出，目前已
有跡象顯示，通膨預期升高，且價格壓力正逐漸擴散至更廣泛的領域。經濟展望
方面，印度內需、投資及基礎建設支出持續支撐景氣，央行將 2026/27 財年經濟
成長率預測由 6.7%上調至 7.1%，另受中東局勢緊張、國際油價上漲、食品價格
攀升及氣候因素影響，將 2026/27 財年 CPI 通膨率預測上調至 5.2%，高於 4%的
中期目標。另外，世界銀行 10 月最新《南亞經濟展望》報告，上修印度 2026/27
財年經濟成長率 0.4 個百分點至 7.1%，反映內需消費、製造業、服務業及出口表
現優於預期，然而，中東衝突導致能源價格上漲、聖嬰現象衝擊農業生產、全球
金融市場波動及資本流動風險，仍可能影響經濟前景。整體而言，印度經濟成長
動能仍穩健，但外部風險及輸入性通膨壓力增加，未來貨幣政策將以維持物價穩
定為優先，並有進一步升息的可能。 
 (3)越南: 2026 年第三季經濟成長年增率達 9.95%，高於上季修正後的 8.81%，並
創 4 年新高，累計前三季經濟成長率達 9.01%。成長動能主要來自製造業與營建
業擴張、外人直接投資持續流入、以及外貿大幅成長。各產業均穩健成長，工業
及建築業成長 12.50%(占 GDP 總值 51.57%)；服務業成長 9.54%(占 43.29%)；農
業成長 4.21%(占 5.14%)。以支出面觀之，民間消費成長 8.96%，固定投資成長
21.39%，貨物和服務出、進口分別成長23.27%、28.75%。展望未來，越南受惠於
全球企業分散供應鏈、AI 產業需求及政府推動基礎建設與數位轉型 ， 經濟前景仍
偏正向。

六、台灣—出口動能強勁，物價壓力與貿易風險並存 
9 月消費者物價指數(CPI)年增率升至 2.73%，創近兩年半新高，已連續 5 個
月高於 2%警戒線，主要係受豪雨影響，蔬果供應減少帶動價格上漲，加上汽機
車減稅效應淡化、中秋節落點不同及國際油價維持高檔，共同推升物價。除蔬果
外，外食費、房租、電費及交通相關費用皆持續上漲，顯示物價壓力仍在，但主
計總處認為整體漲勢仍屬溫和，預估 10 月 CPI 將略為回落至約 2.5%。 
9 月出口額達 872.2 億美元，年增 60.9%，其中資通產品出口倍增、電子零組
件年增 45.3%。財政部指出，在AI 相關投資持續、部分電子產品供應偏緊、新品
上市與旺季拉貨等因素共同挹注下，出口表現明顯升溫；隨著 AI 應用擴大及年
底採購需求增加，全年出口額有望突破 9,000 億美元。 
美國國際貿易委員會 （USITC）於10 月 9 日依據 《1930 年關稅法》 第337 條
款，對特定垂直供電系統及相關產品啟動專利侵權調查，其中台達電、廣達及鴻
海旗下相關企業等遭列名。若 337 條款調查最終認定侵權，相關產品可能遭禁止
輸入美國，後續應關注調查結果及對台灣電源供應與伺服器供應鏈的影響。

M
Update
Delta Electronics Inc.  | Asia Pacific
September  Sales +1% MoM/
+15% YoY
In this report, we focus on Delta's monthly sales, which we
believe could be a catalyst for its share price.
Details:
• September sales were NT$65,400mn (+1% MoM/+15% YoY).
• 3Q26 sales came in at NT$197,059mn (+8% QoQ/+31% YoY), largely in line
with  our estimate at NT$201,379mn (+10% QoQ/+34% YoY), but slightly
below the consensus at NT$208,250mn (+14% QoQ/+39% YoY).

Our view:
• We believe the modest shortfall in 3Q26 revenue  vs. the market expectation
of ~10% QoQ, is due to some component supply shortages and the
customer's pull-in schedule.
• Looking ahead, we expect 4Q26 revenue to grow by double-digit % QoQ on
alleviated component supply and solid demand.
• Regarding the USITC Investigation No. 337, we believe near-term impact
should be manageable, but we will continue to monitor the developments on
this matter (see our report here).

• Stay OW.

Morgan Stanley Research 3
Valuation Methodology and Risks
Delta Electronics Inc. (2308.TW)
Base case: P/E multiple of 30x based on average 2027-28 EPS.                          We believe our target multiple
is justified by the 63% earnings CAGR we estimate for 2025-28, implying a PEG of 0.5, versus
the stock's ~23x P/E and 23% earnings CAGR during 2022-25, which implied a PEG of 1.0.
Risks to Upside
n Faster-than-expected global economic growth

n Falling raw material prices
n Margin improvement
Risks to Downside
n Global economic slowdown
n Ongoing component supply tightness
n Rising raw material prices
n Margin contraction as a result of higher operating expenses or faster ASP erosion

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

Greater China Technology Semiconductors
Asia Pacific
Industry View Attractive
What’s Changed
Nanya Technology Corp.
(2408.TW) From To
Price Target NT$580.00 NT$600.00
DRAM supply constraints continue. Contract renewals drove
Nanya's 3Q beat.  CXMT's G5 milestone strengthens its tech
roadmap, but yield ramp and customer qualification take time.

Nanya 3Q beat; LTAs in spotlight:  Nanya reported 3Q revenue of NT$134bn, +62%
Q/Q, +612% Y/Y, 8% above consensus. 3Q gross profit was NT$114bn, +114% Q/Q,
12% above consensus, implying a GM of 85.7%. 3Q net profit was NT$88bn, +75%
Q/Q, 9% above consensus, implying a net margin of 65.1%. The 3Q Q/Q revenue
growth was driven mainly by ASP increase, which primarily reflects renewal of the
one-year and six-month L TAs, which were previously negotiated when ASPs were

lower; most  expired in 3Q26. Currently L TAs cover 50-70% of output; only 20% of
output is covered by three-year contracts; Nanya intends to extend the L TA
durations for longer-term supply according to customer demand, and there are
discussions on price floors and ceilings for the new contracts.
Nanya management sees continued DRAM shortage in 4Q and expects that ASP
may continue to improve: The supply constraint is the long lead time for new
capacity, and the undersupply is unlikely to be resolved in the near term. Nanya

targets capex  of NT$69.7bn in 2026 and NT$346bn  for the 35.9kwpm capacity of its
new Fab 5A from 2026-29. Management estimates that AI infra currently accounts
for ~20% of its revenue mix, but that could increase to 40-50% by 2029/30.
CXMT updates: On September 7 during Q&A, CXMT reiterated expectations for
tight DRAM supply in 2H26; its response on Apple expressed openness to global
customers without confirming a supply agreement. On September 21, after its G5

mass production announcement, CXMT clarified that G5 sales are not yet at scale,
while yield ramp-up and customer qualification would take time. Based on our
checks, CXMT's HBM3E yield is already 40%, and DRAM wafer input should ramp to
30kwpm by end-2027.
Constrained DRAM supply is the industry consensus: The 4QF26 earnings call for
Micron  (covered by Joseph Moore) highlighted that  persistent supply constraints are
expected through CY2027-28. It cited industry-wide clean room constraints owing to

lengthy construction; most of the increase in Micron's F27 capex (vs. its previous
plan) is allocated to construction, targeting clean room availability in late CY28 and
beyond. Micron also expects rising industry HBM wafer requirements to limit bit
growth, forecasting industry DRAM bit shipment growth of low-20s (%) in CY27-28.
Samsung (covered by Shawn Kim) also expects HBM to absorb nearly 30% of
industry DRAM wafer capacity in 2027, vs ~20% currently.

Stock implications:
• Nanya Tech (OW):  Price target raised to NT$600; 2026e, 2027e, and 2028e
EPS raised  22%, 18%, and 19%, respectively.
•  CXMT (OW): Base case value/price target unchanged at Rmb88.
Morgan Stanley does and seeks to do business with
companies covered in Morgan Stanley Research. As a result,
investors should be aware that the firm may have a conflict of
interest that could affect the objectivity of Morgan Stanley
Research. Investors should consider Morgan Stanley

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.

### Page 5

Morgan Stanley Research 5
Nanya Tech: Valuation Methodology
We raise our price target (base case scenario value) from NT$580 to NT$600: Our
price target reflects a BVPS multiple of 4.11x for 2026 (was 4.77x) versus ROE of 76% for
2026.  We lower our target multiple to reflect recent memory segment volatility. Our
2026e BVPS estimates is raised to NT$146.00 from NT$121.52. We expect DDR4 pricing
strength and L TAs to drive a re-rating.
We raise our bull and bear case values from NT$1,230 and NT$320 to NT$1,270 and NT

$330, respectively, implying 8.70x and 2.26x 2026e BVPS.

### Page 11

M

Morgan Stanley Research 11
CXMT: Valuation Methodology
We keep our base case value/price target unchanged: We continue to apply RI
methodology to derive our price target. The price target did not change despite our
2026/27 earnings estimate increases because the contribution to calculated value from
our RI model comes mostly from the subsequent forecast period  and the continuing value.
Key RI assumptions remain unchanged, including cost of equity of 9.9%, , intermediate

growth rate of 11.0%, terminal growth rate of 3.0%, and cash dividend payout ratio of 51%,
.
Our base case value/price target now implies 14.3x 2027e EPS vs. our prior target multiple
of 18.4x 2027e BVPS, as we see a stronger DRAM market outlook given the continued
supply constraint; we also believe the G5 breakthrough reduces risks around CXMT's
technology roadmap.
Our bull case value remains unchanged at Rmb132; it now implies 21.5x our 2027e EPS.

We adjust our bear case value to Rmb40 from Rmb70 to account for recent volatility; it
now implies 6.5x 2027e EPS.

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

## Document 104: 261014_gs_semicon-west

Source PDF: all report pdf/261014_gs_semicon-west.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-14
Pages: 10

### Page 2

Key Takeaways from Company Meetings

Key Takeaways from Applied Materials Meeting 
Participants: Kevin Moraes, VP , Strategy and Marketing; Sree Kesapragada, VP , 
Advanced Packaging; Mike Sullivan, Head of Investor Relations 
Investor questions mainly focused on clarifications tied to the company’s investor event 
and financial targets. On revenue and capital intensity, management noted that 
semiconductor industry pricing is a key variable that it cannot control - but has high 
confidence in its framework tied to increasing WFE intensity per node - and these 
increases have already been programmatically locked in by customers today. On 
Applied’s target of 200bps of market share gain, management noted the complex set of 
mix effects involved in the calculation, but noted the substantial impact from China 
export control exclusions. Relative to the company’s newly articulated gross margin 
target of 54% by 2030, Applied believes there is the potential for upside to this target - 
but pointed out that the vast majority of its costs are variable in nature. On its optical 
tool portfolio, Applied believes that it can leverage its expertise and market position in 
e-beam technology to expand into this adjacent $9bn market. On AGS, management has 
high confidence in its outlook for mid-teens long term revenue growth - but noted that 
its services attach rate is higher on its newer tools relative to its installed fleet. 
Key Takeaways from Lam Research Meeting 
Participants: Ram Ganesh - VP of Investor Relations 
Consistent with prior commentary, Lam expects 8-10 new fab projects to come online in 
2027 - and currently expects product growth to be driven by DRAM, followed by 
advanced foundry/logic, followed by NAND flash. The company has not specified a 
timeframe for achieving its new long-term gross margin target of ~55%. However, we 
expect this to be achieved over several years, and see it tied more to product mix 
(particularly with new products such as Akara) and overseas operational manufacturing 
efficiencies rather than like-for-like pricing. Management remains constructive on the 
company’s competitive positioning in Logic, DRAM, and advanced packaging - 
particularly as etch and deposition intensity continues to grow with a variety of new 
device structures coming into the mix - including hard masks, surface prep, TSVs, and 
other materials engineering steps. 
Key Takeaways from Teradyne Meeting 
Participants: Roy Chorev - VP & GM of Compute Test Division; Amy McAndrews - VP of 
Investor Relations 
Teradyne announced a strategic partnership with and investment in FormFactor at 
SEMICON West. Teradyne remains committed to an open ecosystem of probe card 
vendors, but believes Form’s strong position in silicon photonics warrants a closer 
relationship that enables Teradyne to commit additional engineering platform resources. 
Regarding its XPU business, Teradyne continues to expect to gain merchant GPU share in 
the long run - and noted that Rubin test times are nearly 2X Blackwell given the near 
doubling in transistor count. Teradyne is also pursuing the tester opportunity at Intel, 
which is focused on increasing its exposure to external vendors (currently using an

Goldman Sachs Americas Technology: SemiconductorsFor the exclusive use of KEVINLU@LENOVO.COM

### Page 3

internal solution) beginning with its foundry business, where Teradyne sees a
medium-term opportunity. In its memory business, Teradyne expects minimal impact
from customer de-specing of HBM as its demand is tied primarily to unit volume rather
than stack height. In co-packaged optics, Teradyne continues to see a very strong
product ramp opportunity, although given the early stages of development, there are
likely to be significant shifts between testing intensity at different insertions during the

manufacturing process.
Key Takeaways from Camtek Investor Meeting
Participants: Lior Aviram - Executive Chairman; Rafi Amit - CEO; Ramy Langer -
President & COO; Moshe Eisenberg - CFO; Eyal Segev - Marketing Director
We attended Camtek’s Investor Meeting at SEMICON. Our key takeaways are as follows:
Long-term financial targets: In conjunction with its investor meeting, Camtek n
pre-announced 3Q26 revenue of $160 mn –$161 mn (above its prior guidance),

guided to ~$180 mn for 4Q26, and provided an initial 2027 revenue outlook of $800
mn based on orders received to date. Management laid out a long-term revenue
target of $1.2 billion in about three years, with a gross margin target of 55%-56%
and operating margin target of 34%-36%.
Advanced packaging remains the key growth driver: Camtek stressed that n
advanced packaging remains its core business, accounting for ~80% of recent
orders, and expects advanced packaging wafer growth to run at about twice the

broader industry rate through 2031 (13% vs. 7% CAGR). Within advanced packaging,
the company expects the high-end of the market (which accounts for the vast
majority of its business) to grow at a ~24% CAGR through 2031, driven by AI, silicon
photonics, denser interconnects, and increasingly complex inspection and metrology
requirements.
Technology and product roadmap centered on inspection and specialized n
metrology: Camtek’s roadmap remains centered on its margin-accretive Hawk and

Eagle products for inspection (~85% of its 2026 mix), as well as its metrology
platforms. Separately, management also noted it is embedding AI into detection and
classification and plans to launch AI-enabled software in 2027 (which it attributed to
its Visual Layer acquisition).
M&A strategy focused on capability acquisition and strategic expansion: Camtek n
noted that it is pursuing both smaller acquisitions that add technology and/or talent
to its core business, as well as larger strategic transactions that broaden its offering,

while emphasizing that its $1.2 bn revenue target and financial model do not assume
meaningful M&A activity.
Key Takeaways from Onto Innovation Meeting
Participants: Brian Roberts - CFO; Sidney Ho - VP of Investor Relations
Onto remains very constructive on the demand outlook through 2H26 and heading into
2027 across a range of demand drivers - with growth likely to accelerate next year given
the growth of incremental market opportunities. In HBM, Onto believes its business is

likely to be minimally impacted by any “de-spec” activity by XPU vendors, as its tool
14 October 2026   3
Goldman Sachs Americas Technology: SemiconductorsFor the exclusive use of KEVINLU@LENOVO.COM
e92c7a75ab8b4efbba794e6b187208c8

### Page 4

demand is far more driven by unit volume than stack height. The company continues to
see CoWoS as a key demand driver that is increasingly driven by OSAT partners rather
than TSMC; Onto sees EMIB-T as a significant opportunity that promises to be material in
2028. From a competitive standpoint, Onto remains highly confident in its market share
position, including at TSMC - and sees no imminent impact from potential product
launches by competitors. On margins, management remains confident in its target of

350bps of gross margin expansion, which it believes can be achieved solely by product
mix and efficiencies in its manufacturing operations. The company believes it has room
to grow revenue substantially from projected levels, and does not see itself as capacity
constrained.
Key Takeaways from MKS Instruments Meeting
Participants: John Lee - President & CEO; Paretosh Misra - VP of Investor Relations
MKS believes the industry is in the early-to-middle stages of a substantial cyclical

industry upturn driven by AI and datacenter demand. The company is currently
preparing for a potential WFE spending envelope of up to $300bn in 2028, and
potentially higher over time. In the near-term, management expects the company to
potentially outgrow its customers (2Q26 was the first time the company delivered Y/Y
Semiconductor revenue growth in excess of them) given supply chain inventory effects.
MKS believes it has enough capacity to support WFE of ~$250bn with its Penang facility

(with a surge capacity of another 20%), but may need to expand capacity further if
these forecasts come to fruition. Importantly, the company does not believe it is
constraining its customers’ growth plans today. MKS has not taken significant pricing
actions, but could do so selectively in order to capture the value it delivers. Management
believes its product portfolio is well positioned, but would consider a possible
divestiture of its specialty industrial product lines at an attractive valuation.

### Page 5

Valuation/Risks

AMAT (Buy, on CL): Our 12-month price target of $670 is based on a 32X P/E multiple 
applied to our normalized EPS estimate of $21.00. Key downside risks include: (1) 
incremental export restrictions; (2) local China-based supplier share gains. 
LRCX (Buy): Our price target is $380, which is based on a 35X multiple applied to our 
normalized EPS forecast of $10.75. Key downside risks: (1) softer DRAM/HBM demand; 
(2) delays in planned clean room and fab expansions; (3) lower-than-expected content 
gains from advanced packaging and 3D architectures. 
TER (Buy): Our 12-month price target is $465, which is based on a 37X P/E multiple 
applied to our normalized EPS forecast of $12.50. Key downside risks: (1) delays in the 
revenue ramp with a merchant GPU customer, (2) significant quarterly volatility due to 
the lumpy nature of large AI-related infrastructure projects, (3) slower-than-expected 
recovery in Mobile. 
ONTO (Buy): Our12-month price target is $400, which is based on 32X our normalized 
EPS estimate of $12.50. Our target P/E is in line with peer multiples, and it is on the 
higher end of ONTO’s historical median, given recent momentum around HPC. Key 
upside/downside risks: (1) additional volume purchase agreements and accelerated 
customer adoption of Dragonfly G5; (2) shift towards OSATs or domestic Chinese 
manufacturers; (3) competitor launching a rival tool. 
CAMT (Neutral): Our 12-m price target of $160 is based on a 38X P/E multiple applied 
to our normalized EPS of $4.20. Key upside/downside risks: (1) notable push-outs in 
chiplet architectures; (2) failure to pass on higher pricing; (3) better-than-expected 
growth in HPC-driven revenue in the medium-term. 
MKSI (Sell): Our 12-month price target is $270, which is based on a 20X P/E multiple 
applied to our normalized EPS of $13.50. Key upside risks: (1) rebound in the NAND 
market from an equipment ordering perspective; (2) greater realization of synergies 
from the Atotech acquisition; (3) any material pickup in momentum for the company’s 
Semiconductor business due to broader WFE trends.

### Page 4

BT substrate supply/demand gap at 11%/14% in 2027/28E on the back of solid memory demand

We introduce our 2026-28E BT substrate TAM forecast, expanding at a 32% 2026-28E 
CAGR to reach US$16.8bn in 2028E (up from US$7.4bn in 2025; Exhibit 1). This growth is 
primarily driven by solid memory demand in general servers (general servers will account 
for 32% of total BT TAM in 2028E, up from 21% in 2025; Exhibit 2), with increasing 
memory capacity/BT content per server. We expect the memory BT substrate TAM to 
outpace the broader BT substrate TAM with a 41% 2026-28E CAGR, accounting for 77% 
of total BT substrate TAM in 2028E (was 56% in 2025). 
On the other hand, we expect the capacity growth rate to decelerate vs. the previous 
upcycle in 2020-22 (6% 2026-28E CAGR vs. 9% 2020-22 CAGR), as key Asian substrate 
suppliers are placing greater focus on their ABF substrate business, and are hence 
scaling back their BT substrate capacity (including Unimicron, which held 8% market 
share in 2025). 
As such, we now expect the overall BT substrate supply/demand gap to reach 
1%/11%/14% in 2H26/2027/2028 (Exhibit 3), while major BT suppliers have started to 
reduce capacity from 2026, potentially leading to a supply shortage in 2H26. We expect 
BT capacity utilization rates (UTR) to trend up amid a supply-constrained environment 
(surpassing 100% from 2027E per GSe), providing a better BT pricing environment going 
forward. The BT substrate supply tightness since the start of the year has directly led to 
a 10% YTD increase in overall BT substrate pricing, and we believe pricing momentum 
for BT substrates will remain robust in 2H26-2028E. 
Accordingly, we forecast overall BT substrate prices will further increase by 
15%-20%/10%-15% in 2027/28E, benefiting BT substrates’ profitability/pricing for 
Taiwanese players (GMs ranged from 15%-27% in 2Q26 and are projected to increase to 
18%-34%/19%-36%+ in 2027/28E; Exhibit 4).

Solid growth in general server BT substrate TAM will support mid- to long-term BT 
market growth 
We expect general server BT substrate TAM to deliver a 42% 2026-28E CAGR and reach 
US$5.3bn in 2028E (up from US$1.6bn in 2025; Exhibit 8), which will account for 32% of 
total BT substrate TAM in 2028E (up from 21% in 2025). We expect general server 
memory BT substrate TAM to grow slightly faster at a 43% 2026-28E CAGR and reach 
US$5.0bn in 2028E (up from US$1.4bn in 2025), accounting for 39% of total memory BT 
substrate TAM in 2028E (up from 20% in 2025). This is driven by solid general server 
shipments (we expect general server shipments to register an 8% 2026-28E CAGR; see 
here; Exhibit 9) alongside increasing memory capacity/BT content per general server 
(Exhibit 10). 
Our US analyst, James Schneider, expects Intel/AMD general server CPU shipment to 
grow at a 28%/36% 2026-28E CAGR, reaching a combined 59mn units in 2028E (vs. 
26mn units in 2025). This supports our constructive view that general servers are the key 
driver of the current BT substrate upcycle, given the strong correlation between memory 
BT substrate TAM growth and general server CPU shipment growth.

### Page 8

Strong AI server shipment growth in 2026-28E will also support BT TAM expansion 
We expect AI server BT substrate TAM to deliver a 73% 2026-28E CAGR and reach 
US$1,476mn in 2028E (up from US$160mn in 2025; Exhibit 14), accounting for 9% of 
total BT substrate TAM in 2028E (up from 2% in 2025). We expect AI server memory BT 
substrate TAM to grow slightly faster at a 75% 2026-28E CAGR and reach US$1,428mn 
in 2028E (up from US$145mn in 2025), accounting for 9% of total memory BT substrate 
TAM in 2028E (up from 2% in 2025). This is driven by solid growth in AI server shipments, 
as we expect AI servers (8-GPU equivalent) and AI server racks (72-GPU equivalent) to 
register 17%/71% 2026-28E CAGRs (see here) with increasing memory capacity per 
server/per server rack. 
We expect GPU/CPU attach rates to move from 2x to 1.1-1.4x over time as agentic AI 
applications become mainstream, implying that each GPU deployment will require more 
server CPUs (see our US analyst Jim Schneider’s Intel initiation). Given that memory BT 
content in AI servers is primarily tied to server CPU count, if the GPU/CPU attach rate 
narrows from 2.0x to 1.1-1.4x, we estimate 1%/1%-2% upside to memory BT substrate 
TAM in 2027/28E. 
For AI server racks (72-GPU equivalent), BT content per rack continues to expand mainly 
due to increasing demand for more memory capacity, resulting in a 50% 2026-28E 
CAGR in BT content per AI server rack (Exhibit 15). A key upgrade is that Nvidia’s VR200 
will support higher DRAM capacity, implying a 100% increase in DRAM memory BT 
substrate content compared to GB200/GB300. We also note Nvidia is releasing a 
standalone Vera CPU rack designed for agentic AI, implying further demand upside for 
AI CPU shipments and memory BT substrates, as each Vera CPU rack incorporates 8x 
more DRAM memory BT substrate content compared to VR200.

### Page 9

Smartphones remain critical to BT demand with growth going forward tracking 
slower than the overall BT industry 
We expect overall smartphone BT substrate TAM to grow at a 24% 2026-28E CAGR 
(tracking slower than the overall BT industry CAGR of 32%) to reach US$6.5bn in 2028E 
(up from US$3.8bn in 2025), accounting for 39% of total 2028E BT TAM (down from 
51% in 2025). We expect smartphone memory BT substrate TAM to grow at a faster 36% 
2026-28E CAGR to reach US$2.4bn in 2028E (up from US$1.0bn in 2025), accounting 
for 19% of total memory BT TAM in 2028E (down from 24% in 2025). 
As mentioned in our previous update on the BT substrate industry (see here), we believe 
the previous upcycle (in 2020-22) was mainly driven by the proliferation of SiP/AiP 
designs that increased BT substrate content per device, especially for smartphones. 
While smartphones remain critical to overall BT demand, accounting for 51% of total 
2025 BT substrate TAM, we expect their contribution to moderate going forward 
(reaching 39% of 2028E BT TAM). This is driven by softer smartphone shipment volumes 
(we now forecast smartphone shipments at -10%/+2%/+2% YoY in 2026-28E; see here) 
and the fact that BT substrate content per smartphone will be mainly driven by higher 
memory BT substrate pricing (Exhibit 16) rather than other meaningful content value 
gains (memory will account for 46% of smartphone BT ASP in 2028E, up from 32% in 
2025). 
We expect 25%/26%/28% of overall smartphone shipments to be equipped with 
mmWave connectivity in 2026/27/28E (Exhibit 17), resulting in an 18% 2026-28E CAGR 
for smartphone AiP BT TAM to reach US$1,128mn in 2028 (up from US$713mn in 2025). 
For iPhones, mmWave connectivity is only available for models sold in the US 
(representing 35%-40% of total iPhone shipments per GSe). In the Android camp, 
Samsung’s flagship smartphones that support mmWave connectivity are also only for 
the US market.

### Page 10

Automotive BT consumption to steadily increase in coming years 
We expect automotive BT TAM to grow at a 34% 2026-28E CAGR and reach US$777mn 
in 2028E (up from US$317mn in 2025), which will account for 5% of total 2028E BT TAM 
(up from 4% in 2025). This stronger-than-industry growth is mainly driven by rising EV 
penetration going forward (EV penetration rate will increase from 14% in 2025 to 20% in 
2028E per our Japanese auto team; Exhibit 19) and the corresponding increase in 
electronics content per car, which requires more BT substrates. Furthermore, we believe 
rising autonomous driving penetration will result in an increase in electronic 
components/BT consumption per car.

BT market revenue to grow at a 28% 2026-28E CAGR with sustainable memory 
demand

US$14.6bn in 2028E (up from US$6.9bn in 2025). Despite this solid growth, total BT 
market revenue will account for less than 1% of customers’ overall revenue in 2026-28E 
(down from 2%-3% in the previous upcycle in 2020-22), as customers’ overall growth is 
tracking faster at a 34% 2026-28E CAGR. We believe this lower ratio of BT market

BT market revenue as a percentage of total customers’ revenue peaked during the

BT supply analysis: Major suppliers starting to reduce capacity, leading to slower capacity growth vs. 
previous upcycle

We believe overall BT industry supply growth in this upcycle will remain modest, as key 
Asian BT substrate suppliers are decreasing capacity, which will partially offset capacity 
additions from smaller players. Thus, we expect supply to grow at a 6% 2026-28E 
CAGR, compared to the 9% CAGR during the 2020-22 upcycle. This is mainly due to 
Unimicron transferring BT substrate capacity to high-end large-body-size ABF 
substrates (see here), while SEMCO is focusing more on expanding its ABF substrate 
capacity (see here) with its BT substrate business mainly focusing on memory and

We expect BT capacity for Taiwanese suppliers to contract at a -1% 2026-28E CAGR,

The BT substrate supply market remains fragmented, with the top 5 suppliers accounting 
for 53% of total industry revenue in 2025. Over the next few years, we expect a slight 
change in the competitive landscape as major suppliers reduce capacity against a 
backdrop of robust demand expanding at a 32% 2026-28E CAGR, as outlined above.

We believe market players will increase BT substrate capacity by only 4%/3%/9% in 
2026/27/28E. Taiwanese players, such as NYPCB and Kinsus, mentioned they will 
primarily seek de-bottlenecking instead of investing in new equipment. However, ZDT 
mentioned it will expand BT capacity at its Qinhuangdao plants 1 & 2 starting in 2H26, 
and the company expects this expansion to increase its existing BT substrate capacity by 
20%. 
As for major mainland Chinese players, Shennan (002916.SZ; Buy; covered by Ting Song) 
will continue to expand its IC substrate business; the company expects Phase 2 of its 
Guangzhou factory to start production in 4Q26 (see here). Fastprint (002436.SZ; Not 
Covered), which will hold 3% of total market share in 2026 based on BBG consensus, has 
announced in June an investment of Rmb1.2bn for further BT capacity expansion; this 
translates to an additional 25k square meter monthly capacity in the coming years (vs. 
its current 50k square meter monthly capacity).

capacity additions. In June, LG Innotek (011070.KS; Neutral; covered by Guini Lee) 
announced plans to invest US$1bn to further expand its substrate capacity in Vietnam, 
mainly for RF-SiP , BT and ABF substrates. For SimmTech (222800.KQ; Not Covered), the 
company plans to invest KRW105bn to expand BT substrate capacity specifically for

### Page 14

We have seen BT substrate demand improve since the start of 2026 alongside a firmer 
pricing outlook, and we expect demand growth to consistently outpace supply in 
2026-28E (demand CAGR of 15% vs. supply CAGR of 6%). We believe industry utilization 
rates (UTR) will continue to trend up over the coming years (reaching 100%+ after 
2027E) due to slow supply growth going forward. 
Memory predominantly requires wire bonding chip scale package (WBCSP) technology, 
and this production capacity is not interchangeable with flip-chip chip-scale package 
(FCCSP) technology (where smartphone APs represent the biggest demand driver). As 
such, we believe memory BT substrate supply will remain tight through at least 1H28, as 
new dedicated memory capacity will only come online in 2028. Overall BT pricing has 
already risen by 10% YTD, partially driven by higher material costs (including substrate 
CCL, copper foil, gold, etc., which account for 60% of total BT substrate BOM; Exhibit 
26). Going forward, we believe the slow increase in BT substrate supply, especially 
WBCSP capacity, will be the key price hike driver rather than simply reflecting the 
material cost increase. Furthermore, BT substrates typically account for less than 5% of 
total memory BOM, providing ample room for further BT price hikes amid the rising 
memory pricing trend.

### Page 15

With favorable BT substrate supply/demand conditions, we believe the profitability of 
key BT vendors will see a structural increase in the coming years. Similar to current 
dynamics in the ABF market, the BT substrate industry’s profitability profile has 
navigated three upcycles and three downcycles over the past 20 years (Exhibit 27). 
Overall margin profiles for key BT vendors have begun improving since the start of 2026, 
supported by increasing memory demand amid a supply-constrained environment, 
especially following several years of slow capex investment. We believe this profitability 
enhancement will continue in 2H26-2028, supported by sustained industry demand 
uptrend.

### Page 16

Optical module mSAP is seeing a widening S/D gap, prompting a shift of BT capacity to meet strong 
demand

We introduce our 2026-28E 800G+ optical module mSAP TAM forecast, expanding at an 
87% 2026-28E CAGR to reach US$7.1bn by 2028E (up from US$0.5bn in 2025; Exhibit 
28). This is based on our 800G+ optical module shipment estimates of a 48% 2026-28E 
CAGR (see here). The connectivity PCB within the 800G+ optical module requires mSAP 
technology, where several PCB makers have exposure to and are able to leverage their 
extensive mSAP experience from smartphone substrate-like PCB (SLP) production. 
On the other hand, major PCB and substrate suppliers are placing greater focus on this 
emerging business and pursuing proactive capacity expansion plans, especially ZDT and 
Compeq (the latter aims to ramp optical module mSAP capacity to reach monthly 
revenue of ~NT$3bn by end 2027 vs. less than NT$100mn as of 2Q26; see here). 
Although Unimicron was a front-runner in the 800G+ optical module mSAP business in 
2025 (29% market share in 2025 per GSe), the company is placing greater focus on its 
ABF substrate business, and hence leaving room for emerging peers to capture market 
share through aggressive expansion plans and technology leadership. 
We believe ZDT will become the market share leader in the optical module mSAP 
industry, as we expect the company to expand mSAP capacity by 5x over the next two 
years, positioning it well to gain share in a supply-constrained environment (our industry 
checks indicate mSAP is the most supply-constrained component in the 1.6T optical 
transceiver supply chain). Furthermore, the company currently holds an industry-leading 
yield of 90%+ for its optical module mSAP products. 
Overall, we expect the 800G+ optical module mSAP supply/demand gap to reach 
5%/25%/38% in 2026/27/28E (Exhibit 29). Although major mSAP suppliers continue 
to have proactive expansion plans, this appears insufficient to keep pace with strong 
demand. The 800G+ optical module mSAP tightness since the start of 2026 has directly 
led to a 20%-30% pricing increase YTD, and we believe the pricing outlook for 800G+ 
optical module mSAP will remain solid in 2027/28E.

We expect BT substrates to account for 29%/21%/18% of NYPCB’s total revenue in 
2026/27/28E, delivering 42%/53%/38% YoY BT revenue growth. In terms of application 
mix, we expect NYPCB to generate 70% of its 2026E BT revenue from memory, followed 
by smartphone-related products at an 18% share. NYPCB stands out from its Taiwanese 
peers with the highest % of BT revenue derived from memory, and we believe memory 
BT substrates will see larger price hikes compared to other BT substrates due to tighter 
supply/demand conditions. As a result, we expect NYPCB to see the largest BT price 
increases among peers with its BT pricing rising 39%/38%/28% in 2026/27/28E, while 
GM for its BT substrate business reaches 25%/34%/36%.

Raising earnings estimates on better BT substrate pricing

### Page 23

Investment thesis, PT methodology and risks

Investment Thesis - Zhen Ding Technology 
Zhen Ding Technology (ZDT) is the global leading PCB supplier (top 1 in market share in 
sales) with 7-10% market share, and started its ABF substrate business from 2023 in 
Shenzhen, China; it has the highest ABF revenue exposure to the China market compared 
to all other ABF substrate suppliers. We believe ZDT will hold more than 40% ABF 
substrate market share in China by 2027E (up from 31% in 2024) and benefit the most 
from the strong and growing China ABF substrate market (53% 2025-27E CAGR) due to 
the increasing in-sourcing ratio for the AI computing ASIC/GPU market in China. We also 
expect the increasing foldable smartphone penetration rate in the long term to drive 
ZDT’s FPC TAM, and account for 39% of the company’s total revenue in 2027E. We see 
further revenue upside potential from foldable phones. We are Buy rated mainly due to 
our positive view on the company’s China ABF substrate business opportunity, which 
should not only drive ZDT’s revenue growth, but also improve its OPM level in the long 
term. We see upside for the stock price considering the company’s solid growth 
opportunity, and our TP implied P/E multiple is still lower than the company’s past 10+ 
year P/E average, which we believe is fair considering the potential upside from China 
ABF substrate market and increasing foldable phone penetration rate.  
Valuation: Our 12-month TP of NT$1,025 is based on a SOTP valuation methodology, 
including (1) 3.5x blended 2028E P/B for ABF & BT substrate, (2) 20x 2028E P/E for 
high-growth AI server/switch related PCB (in line with industry average), and (3) 8x 
2028E P/E for low-growth consumer electronics PCB (in line with industry downcycle 
average valuation). 
Key downside risks include: (1) weaker-than-expected iPhone demand or slower 
content upgrade, (2) delay in new substrate capacity or slower-than-expected yield rate 
ramp up progress, and (3) fiercer competition in China ABF market.

### Page 24

Investment Thesis - Kinsus
Kinsus is one of the key IC substrates suppliers globally with ~10% ABF/BT substrates
market share in 2024, with ~18% of total 2025 revenue contributed by its contact lens
subsidiary, Pegavision (6941.TW, Not Covered). With our view that the ABF substrate
shortage started from 2Q26 (ABF substrate return to supply demand balance from late
2025), we believe Kinsus’ performance will continue to improve compared with high-end

ABF peers considering the company’s increasing market share in the high-end ABF
substrate market; however, ~36% of the company’s revenue was contributed by BT
substrates in 2025 and 30% of the company’s ABF revenue is from non-LTA customers,
which we believe will continue to enjoy a better pricing outlook. As a result, we believe
Kinsus’ earnings outlook should continue to be strong (~118% 2026-29E CAGR), mainly
driven by better BT substrate and non-LTA ABF substrate pricing outlook, despite the

unfavorable demand outlook before 1H26. We believe Kinsus is an experienced
substrate supplier with a clear capacity expansion plan that can also upgrade its product
portfolio in the long term. We believe we are at the beginning of an industry upcycle,
making us positive on Kinsus’ stock performance over the next 12 months.
Price Target Risks and Methodology - Kinsus
Valuation methodology: Our 12-month target price of NT$1,515 for Kinsus is based on

19.5x 2028E P/E, which is +2.0x STDV higher than Kinsus’ past upcycle average P/E
(14.2x).
Key downside risks: (1) Slower-than-expected PC demand recovery, (2)
Slower-than-expected ABF substrates pricing upgrading outlook, (3)
Slower/Less-than-expected AI server orders from 2H26.
Investment Thesis - NYPCB
NYPCB manufactures and markets printed circuit boards (PCBs) and integrated circuit
(IC) substrates (including ABF and BT substrates). We are Buy rated on NYPCB

considering the company’s high exposure to BT substrates and non-LTA ABF substrate
(70%+ of total revenue), where we expect GM/OPM to increase significantly in the
coming quarters (from breakeven in OPM in 1H25 to 26/42% in 2026/27E) due to the
favorable substrate pricing outlook. Despite the company’s low exposure to high-end
ABF substrate industry (<20% of total revenue in 2025), we believe the expanding
demand from ASIC AI server and high-end switch IC demand will drive the company’s

high-end ABF revenue exposure to 40%+ by 2027E, suggesting better GM/OPM outlook
in the long term. In 2H25-2H26, we believe NYPCB will continue to benefit from stronger
ABF/BT pricing and should be a key beneficiary of the pricing uptrend considering the
company’s key focus on BT and non-LTA ABF substrate industries. Moreover, in the long
term (after 2026), we expect the ABF industry to return to supply tightness, and NYPCB’s
expanding high-end exposure will lead to a long-term earnings growth outlook (148%

2026-28E earnings CAGR). As a result, we see solid upside potential for NYPCB’s
earnings and stock performance.
Price Target Risks and Methodology - NYPCB
Valuation methodology: Our 12-month target price of NT$2,700 for NYPCB is based
on 21x 2028E P/E, which is +1.5x STDV higher than the past upcycle average P/E (13.3x).
Key downside risks: (1) Slower-than-expected PC demand recovery, (2)

Slower-than-expected ABF substrates pricing upgrading outlook, and (3)
Slower-than-expected NYPCB new high-end capacity qualification progress.
Investment Thesis - Unimicron Technology
Unimicron is a key supplier of ABF substrates (25% market share in 2025) and BT
substrates and also produces FPC/HDI/PCB products. We are Neutral rated on the
shares given long-term growth opportunity prospects: (1) over 70% of ABF shipments
are covered by long-term agreements (LTA), which limits pricing flexibility and makes

price hike less likely; (2) the company’s execution track record has been weaker than
peers; (3) capacity expansion plan remains slower than peers, constraining its ability to
capture incremental AI demand. The current ABF substrates market is seeing more
favorable supply demand outlook, and Unimicron is likely to see better pricing outlook
to reflect rising material costs in the coming quarters. AI ABF account for 10% of the
company’s total revenue, but the growth is likely to be slower than peers due to the

company continuing to lose market share to peers given its less favourable production
yield and slow capacity expansion. Overall, we are Neutral rated on Unimicron given our
outlook for limited upside potential, barring ASIC AI servers, despite our generally
positive view in the long term on its profitability trajectory.
Price Target Risks and Methodology - Unimicron Technology
Valuation methodology: Our 12-month target price of NT$1,270 for Unimicron is

based on 17.4x 2028E P/E, which is 2.7x STDV higher than the past upcycle average P/E
(10.3x).
Key upside/downside risks: (1) Slower/faster-than-expected PC demand recovery, (2)
Slower/faster-than-expected ABF substrates upgrading pace, and (3) Slower/faster
than-expected AI server PCB market share loss progress.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.

開始貢獻先進製程 MA 收入，加以 AI 晶片及矽光子檢測需求顯著升

億元 ，+19.6%QOQ，+31.6%YOY， 投顧估計毛利率將大幅優於2Q，
3Q26 EPS 1.81 元。 
 AI、矽光子、海外市場、矽光子分析設備等多動能齊發，預期 2027
年營運明顯走升：汎銓今年每月營運成本大致穩定在約 2.1 億元內，
27 年每月營運成本約 2.16 億元，只要營收持續成長，獲利將迅速放
大。為因應 AI 需求，AI 專區第三期擴產於 7 月完成並開始貢獻營
收，且計畫在年底前開始第四期擴產，對下半年業務樂觀看待，尤其
海外業績成長極為迅速 ， 預計到今年底 ， 海外業務佔比將有望達將近
40%。另外，汎銓已成功自主研發並組裝完成 3 台矽光子分析設備，
其核心光損偵測裝置技術已順利取得台灣、日本、美國及韓國專利。
標準版設備 9 月開放客戶驗證，11/5 正式對外實機展示，今年底開
始銷售，預期今年首批有 2~3 台設備可出貨，明年目標出貨 10 台，
標準機台售價約 6,000 多萬元 ， 此設備毛利率預期在50%以上水準，
若額外選配各項高階測試模組，單台售價可提升至 1 億至 3 億元。

汎銓 主力為 MA （材料分
析 ） ， 營 收 佔 比超 八 成  
 汎銓成立於 2005 年 7 月，為檢測分析服務公司，協助客戶解決先進製程中遇到
的研發與品質問題，以提高客戶的生產效率，尤其在 AI 晶片、先進封裝、矽光
子領域有深厚布局 ，此外由於材料分析檢測交期平均在三天內、 故障分析檢測交
期在一周內，成為其競爭優勢。以營收比重來分，材料分析（MA）佔 2026 前 7
月營收比重 84％，IC 故障分析（FA）營收佔比 8％、矽光子+AI 晶片營收佔比
8％。 
汎銓有九個 MA 實驗室，三個 FA 實驗室，一個 RA 實驗室。2Q26 台灣營收佔
比 65%， 美國 、 日本 、 大陸等海外營收佔比35%， 目前全球員工人數共869 人。

預期 3Q26 毛利率 大幅 優於 前
期，EPS 1.81 元 
 汎銓 2Q26 營收 6.35 億元，+9.8%QOQ，+16.6%YOY，隨 AI 晶片檢測分析等
高毛利業務需求強勁，毛利率回升至 24.96％，季增 3.4 個百分點，費用率降至
22.4%， 稅後EPS 0.35 元 ， 營業利益金額 1611 萬元 ， 稅後淨利金額 1860 萬元。
汎銓 3Q 營收 7.59 億元，+19.6%QOQ，+31.6%YOY，投顧估計毛利率將大幅
優於 2Q，3Q26 EPS 1.81 元。

AI 、 矽 光 子 、 海 外 市 場 、矽
光 子 分 析 設 備 等 多動能 齊
發 ， 預期 20 2 7 年 營運 明顯
走升  
 汎銓表示今年每月營運成本大致穩定在約 2.1 億元內，27 年每月營運成本約
2.16 億元，只要營收持續成長，獲利將迅速放大。今年 3 月起各個新建廠區已
開始貢獻業績，公司運營已從 3 月開始轉虧為盈。為因應 AI 需求，AI 專區第
三期擴產於 7 月完成並開始貢獻營收，且計畫在年底前開始第四期擴產 ，對下
半年業務樂觀看待，尤其海外業績成長極為迅速，預計到今年底，海外業務佔
比將有望達將近 40%。投顧預期汎銓 4Q26 營收季增 8.6%，年增 39.2%，估
計 4Q26 稅後 EPS 2.8 元。 
另外，汎銓已成功自主研發並組裝完成 3 台矽光子分析設備，其功能即是協助
客戶進行光損及漏光定位，並開發從晶片背面進行收光偵測的技術，鎖定矽光
子常用的 1310nm、1550nm 波段，協助定位 PIC 元件、光耦合及封裝過程中
的異常位置，且其核心光損偵測裝置技術已順利取得台灣、日本、美國及韓國
專利。對汎銓更具戰略意義的是，將不再侷限於檢測分析服務供應商的角色，
而是應客戶強烈需求，規劃進一步推出適用於量產端與品質驗證使用的矽光子
測試設備。標準版設備 9 月開放客戶驗證，11/5 正式對外實機展示，今年底開
始銷售，預期今年首批有 2~3 台設備可出貨，明年目標出貨 10 台，標準機台
售價約 6,000 多萬元，此設備毛利率預期在 50%以上水準，若額外選配各項高
階測試模組，單台售價可提升至 1 億至 3 億元。汎銓近年積極建立 AI 晶片分析
平台，並同步發展矽光子檢測、自主設 備開發與專利授權等新成長動能，期望

### Page 1

SK 스퀘어 (402340/KS) 
하이닉스 자사주 소각 이후가 더 기대 
예정보다 빠른 SK 하이닉스 자사주 매입 
SK스퀘어의 자회사인 SK하이닉스는 지난 8월 20일부터 11월 19일까지 자사주 40
조원(2,407만주, 3.3%)을 매입 예정이다. 9월 29일까지 1,715만주, 예정 매입수량 
대비 71.3%를 매입 완료하였다. 매입 초기 매일 65만주 매입하다가 9월 10일부터는 
60만주를 매입하고 있는데, 이러한 추세가 유지된다면 10월 중순경 자사주 매입이 마
무리될 예정이다. 자사주 매입 후 소각 예정으로 소각이 완료되면 SK스퀘어의 SK하아
닉스에 대한 지분율은 20.0%에서 20.7%로 상승하게 된다.

자사주 매입 이후 추가적인 주주환원에 대한 기대 
SK하이닉스는 지난 8월 19일 정책기간(‘25년~’27년) 동안 발생하는 누적 FCF의 
50% 이상을 주주환원 하는 주주환원 정책을 발표한 바 있다. 26년 SK하이닉스의 예
상 FCF는 164조원(컨센서스 기준)으로 50%를 주주환원 할 경우 자사주 매입 금액
(40 조원)을 제외한 주주환원 재원은 42 조원이다. 잔여 주주환원 재원이 모두 배당으
로 지급된다면 SK 하이닉스 자사주 소각 이후 SK 스퀘어의 지분율은 20.7%이므로 
SK스퀘어의 배당수입도 8.7조원에 달한다. 지난 3월 SK스퀘어는 ‘26년~’28년 경
상배당수입의 30% 이상을 주주에게 환원하는 정책을 발표했다. 보수적으로 30%만 
가정하더라도 SK스퀘어의 배당재원은 2.6조원(현 시가총액 대비 1.8%)이다. SK스퀘
어는 연초 주주총회에서 배당가능이익 확보 차원에서 자본준비금 5 조 8,900억원을 
이익잉여금으로 전입한 바 있다. 이는 26년 재무제표가 확정되는 27년 3월 이후 배당
가능이익으로 편입되므로 SK스퀘어의 주주환원 확대가 기대된다.

투자의견 매수, 목표주가 185 만원(유지) 
SK스퀘어의 순자산 가치 중 98% 이상이 SK하이닉스 지분가치이다. SK하이닉스는 
자사주 매입 이후 17.7% 주가가 상승했으나, SK 스퀘어 주가는 11.4% 상승했다. SK
하이닉스 자사주 매입·소각은 SK 스퀘어의 보유 지분가치 상승뿐 아니라 지분율도 상
승하기 때문에 자사주가 소각될 경우 SK스퀘어의 주가가 탄력적으로 움직일 가능성이 
높다. SK스퀘어 현재 NAV 대비 할인율은 43.7%, 12MF PBR 1.4배로 7월 이후 주
가 조정으로 밸류에이션 매력도 높아졌다. 투자의견 매수와 목표주가 185만원을 유지
한다.

2026-09-30

지주회사/통신서비스/ESG. 최관순 / ks1.choi@sks.co.kr / 3773-8812

## Document 217: Advanced economies _ Resilience to shock after shock (2026-09-24)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Advanced economies  _ Resilience to shock after shock (2026-09-24).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-09-24
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

3
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoPerspectives / 3rd Quarter 2026 / September 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
History tends to repeat itself in advanced economies. Once again, growth ultimately fell short of expectations by only a small
margin in the first half of 2026, despite the conflict in Iran. As early as 2025, the impact of tariffs was less severe than feared.
This is a sign that structural factors (notably AI, defence and electrification) are underpinning growth in these countries

and are expected to continue to do so in 2027. However, there are significant risks to the economic outlook, primarily of a
geopolitical nature, with a significant upside risk to inflation and interest rates. The other instance of history repeating itself
concerns a period that those under 20 cannot have experienced: the period leading up to the 2008 crisis, echoes of which can
be seen in terms of growth, inflation, the direction of monetary policy, and long-term interest rates.

Advanced economies are experiencing trends similar to those they
faced nearly 20 years ago:
- Growth remains resilient in 2025-26 despite shocks, as it did in 2006-
07, in the major advanced economies.
- Inflation is above central banks’ targets: as in 2006-07, this calls for a
tightening of monetary policy.
- In Europe and the United States, long-term interest rates have re -
turned to their 2006-07 levels (1996 in Japan): a challenging environ-

ment for public finances in a context where public debt ratios are signi-
ficantly higher than those seen 20 years ago.
THE GLOBAL ECONOMY IN THE MIDST OF A GROWTH CYCLE
We are already well into 2026, and the picture so far is clear: glo -
bal growth is proving resilient. Following a US tariff shock last year,
growth is once again proving, for the time being, to be significantly less
affected by the conflict in Iran than had been anticipated when it first

broke out. Initially, however, there were fears that this new shock would
hit growth even harder. As with the tariff hike in 2025, the impact was
measurable, particularly in terms of consumer confidence. But in both
2025 and 2026, growth has also shown a degree of resilience.
The figures for the second quarter bear this out, despite the sharp rise
in oil prices, the recessionary impact of which is not yet apparent.
According to our forecasts, growth was expected to reach or exceed 1%

in 2026 in the four major advanced economies (the Eurozone, Japan,
the United Kingdom and the United States, where it exceeds 2%) for
the second consecutive year. This had not happened (excluding the
post-COVID catch-up period) since 2006-07. Growth figures are more
moderate (particularly for demographic reasons), but the private sec -
tor is not (at present) showing the same excesses (at that time, the
credit boom had preceded the worst crisis since the 1930s); although

debt levels have recently risen in the US tech sector.
The driving force behind the current growth cycle is clear: artificial
intelligence (AI). It is fuelling global trade and business investment
in most countries. This is clearly the case in the United States, where
business investment is growing strongly as investment plans in the
US tech sector are implemented.
However, the world’s major economies are currently engaged in a
broader push to expand their capital goods investment. Alongside the

ongoing development of services—particularly digital services—we
have seen a rebound in industrial production in most countries since
early 2025. The leading sectors are primarily technology equipment,
machinery, aeronautics, defence and their essential inputs (energy and
metals). This trend is evident everywhere, including in Europe, and
is becoming entrenched over the long term, with an improvement in
business climate indicators, particularly new export orders. Growth is

therefore expected to strengthen further in 2027, particularly in the
Eurozone, where German investment plans are set to gather momen -
tum and whose spillover effects will have a greater impact on the eco-
nomy.
However, not all sectors are experiencing the same momentum. This
is particularly true for the sectors that depend on household consump-
tion, which has shown mixed trends. Our observation that consumer
spending is more buoyant in the United States than in Europe was

confirmed in 2026, and the outlook does not point to a change in this
ranking.
EDITORIAL
RESILIENCE TO SHOCK AFTER SHOCK

### Page 2

4
EcoPerspectives / 3rd Quarter 2026 / September 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
The bank
for a changing
world
While European and US households are experiencing more or less the
same energy price shock, inflation remains higher in the United States.
But this has not prevented consumer spending from keeping pace, pri-
marily because it is driven by the wealthiest households, who are less
sensitive to inflation than to the wealth effect. Furthermore, while the

savings rate remains high in the Eurozone, US households have been
drawing down their savings. This is also true of British households,
which explains why the UK has seen slightly stronger growth since
the beginning of the year. In Japan, government spending has partially
offset energy inflation (while other countries are doing so to a much
lesser extent than in 2022), which has supported consumption growth.
However, these buffers—reduced savings or fiscal support—have their

limits when shocks persist. The two major current conflicts (Iran and
Ukraine) are far from being resolved, and their impact is continuing.
This persistent geopolitical risk and its implications for inflation repre-
sent a significant headwind to the growth outlook for 2027.
HIGHER INFLATION CALLS FOR MONETARY TIGHTENING
Inflation is exceeding the target set by three of the four central banks
examined in this publication, which is another similarity (albeit with

different underlying causes) with the 2006-07 period. Japan is the ex -
ception, as government support measures for households are tempo -
rarily holding back inflation.
Apart from the Bank of Japan, which is engaged in a long-term norma-
lisation process, no central bank was expected to raise its key rates
in 2026. But they are now being prompted to do so for reasons that
vary from one region to another. The first is the conflict in Iran and
the resulting rise in oil prices, which triggered a rebound in energy

inflation from the second quarter onwards. However, since the conflict
was not resolved by the memorandum of understanding signed in June,
and given that the subsequent drop in oil prices proved short-lived,
inflation is expected to rise again. The rise in gas prices is expected to
exacerbate the impact, particularly in Europe, where inventories are
being replenished ahead of winter. Electricity prices are expected to
follow suit in most countries, but at a more moderate pace than in

2022. Indeed, gas is now used less to generate electricity than it was
at that time (thanks to the expansion of renewable energy), and to an
even lesser extent in France.
There is a significant risk that energy inflation will spread further to
other components of inflation in the future, and inflation expectations
could eventually reflect this. This is why the European Central Bank
has already raised its key interest rate twice (in June and September)

and, according to our scenario, will likely do so again in December. For
the same reason, we expect the Bank of England to raise its policy rate
in November. Although the cap on energy prices was maintained for
a long time, it has now been raised. As a result, inflation is expected
to rise between now and the end of the year. The aim will then be to
prevent second-round effects.
Inflation can also be a sign of (too much) economic strength. In the

United States, it has exceeded the 2% target since 2021. The main
argument in favour of a new cycle of rate hikes (following the one in
2022-2023) lies in the current resilience of growth and the improving
labour market. The Federal Reserve (Fed) must balance two equally
important objectives: maximum employment and price stability. Howe-
ver, unlike in the 4 th quarter of 2025, when the decline in job crea -
tion justified monetary easing (by 75 basis points), the current sharp

rebound in job creation completely reverses that analysis. According
to our forecasts, the unemployment rate is expected to decrease in
the coming months, which pushes back the prospect of a decline in
core inflation. This situation persists due to both supply constraints
(over which a central bank has no control) and excess demand (which
the Fed can, however, mitigate). Consequently, the Fed raised its key
rate in September and is expected to implement two further rate hikes

between now and January 2027.
In Japan, finally, while headline inflation has been brought down by
government support measures, core inflation remains significant.
Wage pressures are mounting against a backdrop of labour shortages.
The Bank of Japan is therefore being forced to accelerate the pace of its
monetary normalisation.
In all these regions, inflation is currently expected to peak in Q4 2026
or Q1 2027. It is against this backdrop of temporary inflation that rate

hikes could also come to an end by that time (excluding Japan). Howe-
ver, this assumes that once the energy shock—the course of which
remains uncertain—subsides, inflation will fall. The effects of El Niño,
which are still difficult to gauge at present, could take over. Inflation
would then remain above the central banks’ target for a longer period.
Under this scenario, rate hikes would go further than is currently en -
visaged.
THE RISE IN LONG- TERM INTEREST RATES: A FACTOR THAT WILL WEIGH

ON PUBLIC FINANCES
Monetary policies have also to offset the inflationary effects of fiscal
policy, particularly in the United States (where the budget deficit is
high and shows no signs of shrinking) and in Japan (where the new
government is pursuing pro-growth policies). In Europe, fiscal policy
is playing a much smaller role than it did in 2022. While the stimulus
measures implemented at that time did help reduce the inflation rate
in the short term (by nearly 2.5 pp in France, for example), by keeping

demand at a high level, they delayed disinflation and increased the
scale of the monetary tightening required.
The outbreak of the conflict in Iran acted as a catalyst: it triggered a
rise in long-term rates (which had been trending downwards at the
start of the year) to levels close to those of 2006-07. This rise is wides-
pread, but its causes vary. Inflation expectations appear to play a more
significant role in the Eurozone, whereas the rise in real interest rates

seems to be the main factor in the United States and Japan.

The bank
for a changing
world
Higher inflation and more resilient real growth than feared point in
the same direction: stronger than expected nominal growth that is li -
kely to be sustained. However, in the long term, high nominal growth
tends to drive up nominal interest rates (as it is a positive sign of more
profitable investment projects), in contrast to the problem of secular

stagnation raised in the 2010s (which combined low nominal growth
with low nominal interest rates).
The other issue is the high level of fiscal deficits (too high for a period
of growth), with various situations. In several countries, the public
debt-to-GDP ratio continues to rise. In the United States, deficit reduc-
tion does not appear to be a priority, to the extent that it is expected
to remain above 6% of GDP at the very least until the end of Donald

Trump’s term of office (with little hope that the mid-term elections will
significantly alter this trend). In the United Kingdom and France, fiscal
consolidation has begun, but it is being hampered by the simultaneous
increase in certain areas of expenditure (debt servicing, defence and
healthcare, due in particular to an ageing population), which is slowing
the pace of consolidation. In Germany, the implementation of invest -
ment plans is leading to a widening of the deficit. While this does not

pose a sustainability problem, it does result in an increase in annual
debt issuance. In Japan, the budget deficit is kept in check by interest
payments that remain low, as the rise in interest rates is recent and
the average maturity of the debt is high (10 years). However, expansio-
nary fiscal policy could pose further problems in terms of debt dyna -
mics if it were to continue through the end of the decade. In Italy and
Spain, fiscal consolidation has been sufficient to stabilise the public

debt-to-GDP ratio (and even to reduce it in Spain), but the high level of
Italy’s debt service it vulnerable to a rise in interest rates.
In all these countries, the fiscal arithmetic is simpler today than it
will be in 2030. Indeed, in most cases, only half of the debt was is -
sued after 2022 (that is, after the end of the zero-interest-rate period).
The debt service has so far only partially risen, except in the United
States, where most of the increase has already occurred due to a shor-

ter average debt maturity (6 years). This means that, for all countries
combined, the rate of GDP growth remains higher than the average
interest rate paid on public debt (or the effective interest rate).
By 2030, the effective rate will have risen and caught up with
GDP growth. As a result, in most cases, the debt service will have re -
turned to the level as a share of GDP seen in the early 2000s. In order to
stabilise public debt-to-GDP ratios, primary balances will need to have

improved. This will even require the generation of primary surpluses,
particularly in Italy (which is already achieving this), France and the
United Kingdom. This is a crucial shift that governments will need to
introduce as early as the 2027 budget; otherwise, they risk postponing
the stabilisation of their public debt-to-GDP ratio once again.
Article completed on 17 September 2026
Stéphane Colliac
stephane.colliac@bnpparibas.com
